# HomeProjectWise — AI Collaboration Handoff

Purpose: This is the ONE AND ONLY shared operations room for the owner, ChatGPT and every Claude session. All cross-assistant instructions, claims, status updates, decisions, handoffs and review requests must be recorded here. Do not create or use a second operations-room document, parallel handoff page, or PR-comment thread as an alternative communication channel. Specialized files (WORK_LOG, PROJECT_STATE, SEARCH_METRICS, article queue, specs and PRs) remain evidence/source records only; they are not competing operations rooms. This file does not replace code review, tests or specialized data records.

## One-command start trigger (2026-10-24)

The owner's trigger phrase is **"غرفة العمليات"**. It applies equally to Claude and ChatGPT.

When the owner sends this phrase by itself, or says "غرفة العمليات — أكمل العمل", do not ask the owner to restate the project context. Start the standard read-before-work procedure above: read this file, the latest `data/WORK_LOG.md`, `data/PROJECT_STATE.md`, and current open PRs including changed files and checks; then read the latest assistant handoff and resume the highest-priority task that is not blocked or overlapping.

If the owner includes a specific task after the phrase, handle that task only after the same checks. If the phrase is sent to Claude, Claude follows this same protocol; if sent to ChatGPT, ChatGPT follows it. The phrase does not wake the other assistant or transfer hidden chat context. Each assistant must write meaningful handoffs to this repository, and must mark unresolved conflicts **BLOCKED** rather than guess.

For tiny independent tasks, no separate CLAIM entry is needed: use the PR description and one `WORK_LOG.md` line. Reserve explicit CLAIM/HANDOFF entries for cross-assistant overlap, protected file boundaries, or work spanning sessions. Avoid concurrent edits to this file; each assistant should edit only its designated section if such sections are introduced, and coordinate durable log changes through sequential PRs.

## Working agreement

1. **Read before acting.** At the start of a session, review this file, the latest entries in `data/WORK_LOG.md`, `data/PROJECT_STATE.md`, and all open PR titles/files/statuses. Do not rely on a prior chat summary when repository state can be checked.
2. **Claim a small task before editing.** State the intended change and target files in the PR description or a dated note here. Avoid files changed by another open PR. If a collision is unavoidable, coordinate explicitly and do not silently overwrite.
3. **Review each other's work constructively.** Look for correctness, reader value, accessibility, performance, source quality, safety, SEO, duplication, category fit, and regressions. Record only actionable advice: observation, evidence, priority, suggested next step. Do not invent findings or claim live verification without checking.
4. **Independent work continues.** A pending reply from the other assistant is not a reason to stop work on a separate, low-risk task. Leave clear handoff notes for anything blocked or needing a second opinion.
5. **Quality before cadence.** Never publish thin or duplicate content to satisfy a daily target. Preserve editorial boundaries, truthful dates, working links, safe instructions, privacy, and trust.
6. **Checks and deployment.** Run the relevant available checks, inspect their actual results, and distinguish PR checks from live-site verification. Do not claim a merge or deployment unless the repository and live page confirm it.
7. **Protected areas.** Do not edit Awin links, `.editorial-freeze`, or `.github/workflows` without explicit owner authorization. Do not expose secrets. New CSS colors require a dark-mode counterpart and contrast checking.
8. **Canonical logging.** Each finished code/content task adds one line to `data/WORK_LOG.md` in its PR. If that file is conflicted by other open PRs, do not force the edit: record the task and the logging conflict in this handoff file/PR, then reconcile the canonical log after the conflicting PRs merge.
9. **No false consensus.** Notes here are proposals, not automatic approval. The assistant implementing a suggestion must validate it independently and explain why it was accepted, adapted, or declined.

## Note format for future handoffs

Add a dated section when there is a meaningful cross-assistant finding:

- **Finding:** specific, evidence-based observation.
- **Impact / priority:** reader, reliability, safety, performance, or project effect.
- **Recommendation:** smallest useful next step.
- **Status:** proposed / accepted / implemented / declined, with PR or commit link where available.

Keep this file concise. The PR discussion is the place for detailed review of a specific change; this file is for durable cross-task context and advice.

## Operations room — mandatory turn-by-turn handoff (started 2026-10-24)

This file is the shared operations room for Claude and ChatGPT. The owner should not need to copy messages between assistants. Each assistant must use this shared file and GitHub state as the handoff channel whenever the owner switches to it.

### Before every work session
1. Read this file first, then the latest data/WORK_LOG.md entries, data/PROJECT_STATE.md, and the current open PRs including their changed files and check status. Do not trust a previous chat summary when live repository state can be checked.
2. Read the latest Operations Room entries below. Identify the other assistant's latest plan, completed work, blocked items, requested review, and explicit file/article exclusions.
3. If the other assistant has marked a task as IN PROGRESS on the same file, do not edit that file. Choose a separate task or leave a review note. If its status is unclear, assume no permission to overlap and ask via this file, not via the owner.
4. Before editing, append a short dated CLAIM note here with owner, task, target files, expected result, and any known overlap. Keep the change small. This is a coordination claim, not a substitute for a PR or work-log entry.
5. Re-check open PRs and file overlap immediately before creating/editing a branch. Never change files in an open PR from a separate branch unless explicitly coordinated.

### When finishing or pausing
- Add a dated HANDOFF note here with status (DONE, IN PROGRESS, or BLOCKED), PR/commit links, exact files changed, checks actually run, live verification (if any), and the next recommended action.
- Tell the other assistant what is needed from it: review, decision, follow-up, or simply awareness. Also state files/articles that must remain untouched until the handoff is cleared.
- The canonical data/WORK_LOG.md still gets one line per completed task in its own PR. If concurrent log edits conflict, preserve every valid line and reconcile sequentially after merge; never overwrite or silently omit entries.
- The latest dated entry from each assistant is its durable message. When the owner switches assistants, the newly activated assistant reads the latest entries and continues from there without requiring the owner to relay messages.
- Do not assume the other assistant is running in the background, can be awakened, or has seen a new entry. The shared file works when that assistant next starts a session and reads it. No claim of real-time notifications.
- If a task needs the other assistant's approval, leave it BLOCKED — awaiting review and work on a non-conflicting task instead of silently proceeding.

### Image pipeline (owner decision, 2026-10-25)
Goal: the owner only types the trigger phrase at the start of a session; the assistants pass images to each other through this file and GitHub.
1. Whoever needs a photo (usually Claude, for a new guide) adds an IMAGE REQUEST entry below: article slug, exact repository path and file name (website/images/<name>.jpg), format JPG 16:9 at 1600x900, ideally under about 300 KB, the generation prompt (no logos, no readable text), the alt text, and the branch to commit to.
2. ChatGPT generates the image and commits it directly to that branch and path, then adds an IMAGE READY entry with the path and commit link. If it cannot generate the image or commit a binary file in that session, it adds IMAGE BLOCKED with the reason, and the owner uploads the file once through the GitHub upload page.
3. Claude wires the image into the article, opens the PR, checks CI, merges and verifies the live page. Claude cannot move an image file from a chat into GitHub (its chat files and its GitHub tools run in separate environments), so it never reports an image as uploaded until it has seen the file in the repository.
4. No image is published without descriptive alt text. Existing images are replaced only on an individually confirmed replace verdict.

Why this one: our existing queries show demand and weak positions for «bathroom vent condensation» (39), «condensation on air vents in bathroom» (80), «bathroom ceiling condensation» (62-88); it is a distinct problem from choosing a fan (choose-bathroom-exhaust-fan) and from general condensation (reduce-bathroom-condensation), so no cannibalization if the intro states the exact symptom. Category: WEEKEND PROJECTS · BATHROOM VENTILATION (Weekend Projects becomes 11; the guide after this one goes to Home Design to rebalance).
- Causes to verify with primary sources BEFORE writing (no claim without a source; drop any cause we cannot source): warm humid air condensing in a cold or uninsulated duct; duct sloping back toward the fan instead of toward the outlet; disconnected or leaking duct; stuck or missing backdraft damper or blocked termination cap; fan too weak or run time too short; and not condensation at all: a roof or flashing leak (simple test: drips during rain with nobody showering). Include a decision block «drips after showers, in rain, or always?» linking to where-to-put-water-leak-sensors; safety (power off at the breaker, attic safety, when to call a roofer or electrician); a 10-minute quick win; US, UK and EU note; sources from Building America Solution Center, ENERGY STAR, HVI, code text and fan makers' installation manuals where they say something checkable.
- Rules it must meet (quality gate and project rules): 900+ words, 8+ H2, 4 images (AI hero via IMAGE REQUEST + 3 original SVG: why a cold duct drips; duct slope and insulation; shower-vs-rain decision), 4+ FAQ, 3+ internal links, intro 130 words or less, alt and caption share a 4+ letter word with the preceding H2, datePublished and dateModified, JSON-LD, GoatCounter script, Keep going and Sources, dark-mode safe, one problem and one category. #### 2026-11-10 — OWNER INSTRUCTION: ONE EDITORIAL QUEUE AND BATCHED CLAUDE HANDOFF

The canonical article pipeline is `docs/ARTICLE-PRODUCTION-QUEUE.md`. ChatGPT owns research and briefs; Claude receives only fully researched READY FOR CLAUDE items in one ordered batch, reviews them against the Operating Charter and current repository, then completes implementation, QA, PR and deployment verification. Do not send fragmented commands or choose unresearched titles directly from the 100-candidate portfolio.

The queue is subordinate to the Operating Charter, Value Before Publishing, the current inventory and owner decisions. It aims for 2–3 valuable articles/day when qualified topics exist, with no forced quota. GSC signals are used to expand promising topic clusters, not to orbit one URL for one or two clicks. Each brief requires query/intent mapping, GSC evidence where available, current US SERP review, 3–5 competitor-page reviews, overlap/cannibalization audit, defensible differentiation, sources/safety, practical outline, word-count rationale (no quota), image plan (0–2 purposeful assets), acceptance tests and the charter scorecard. Minimum 85/100 to enter READY FOR CLAUDE; critical safety/factual/duplicate failures cannot be offset by score.

Initial research sequence starts with Smart Home candidates SH-001 through SH-005; they remain validation candidates, not approved publications. Research SH-001 first and confirm whether SH-002 can be handled inside the existing smart-lighting URL before proposing a new page.
#### 2026-11-10 — OWNER STRATEGY UPDATE — ARTICLE GROWTH AND QUALITY

This owner instruction supersedes any earlier wording that implies we should primarily revisit existing articles simply because they have received a click or two, or that new articles should be deferred until all near-page-one pages have been optimized.

- **Growth model:** use GSC impressions/clicks and query/page patterns to identify promising TOPICS and expand those topic clusters. Do not orbit an individual article because it earned 1–2 clicks. Example: smart-home signals should trigger research into adjacent smart-home questions and practical subtopics, not repeated edits to the same smart-plug article.
- **Publishing target:** aim for **2–3 researched, high-value articles per day** when the qualified backlog and review capacity support it. The long-term ambition is a large, useful library comparable in breadth to strong competitors. Article volume is a strategic goal, but never a reason to publish thin, repetitive or unsupported pages. If fewer than 2–3 topics pass the gate on a given day, publish fewer and use the time to improve the pipeline/backlog.
- **Mandatory pre-writing study for every article:** primary query and close variants; search intent; US SERP review; leading competitor pages and their coverage; what they do well; a specific reader problem and a defensible gap/advantage for HomeProjectWise; overlap/cannibalization against the existing site; sources and practical solution plan. Record keyword/query mapping and evidence in the brief or research notes.
- **Length policy:** no arbitrary large word-count target and no padding. Study the scope and useful coverage of credible competing pages, then write only as much as needed to solve the reader's problem thoroughly. Completeness, clarity, accuracy and utility outrank word count.
- **Images:** use one or two genuinely strong, relevant images/diagrams when they materially explain the task, comparison, layout or decision. No minimum image-count quota. Prioritize visual accuracy, clarity, quality, relevance and correct alt text; never add decorative or misleading images just to satisfy a count. Do not replace existing images without evidence that the current image is mismatched, weak in context or technically defective.
- **Existing articles:** improve them when evidence shows a meaningful content, factual, UX or conversion gap, or when the change strengthens a topic cluster. Do not make near-page-one optimization the default strategy at the expense of creating new, differentiated coverage.
- **Quality gate remains mandatory:** Value Before Publishing is unchanged. No invented data, fabricated first-hand experience, unsupported claims, duplicate intent pages or publication merely to hit cadence. Every article must offer a real solution and a clear reason to choose it.
- **Initial expansion direction:** investigate the Smart Home cluster first because current GSC data shows early search traction for smart-plug suitability and motion lighting. Treat this as a hypothesis to validate with query data and US SERPs, not proof that every smart-home subtopic has demand.
- **Agent division:** ChatGPT should perform topic discovery, keyword/intent research, competitor/SERP analysis, overlap checks, briefs, source verification and editorial drafting where possible. Reserve Claude for complex implementation, technical review, and independent final checks that materially benefit from its expertise. Batch Claude requests to conserve quota.

## 2026-11-11 — OWNER DIRECTIVE: SINGLE CANONICAL OPERATIONS ROOM (supersedes older communication wording)

**Owner concern:** The owner uses more than one Claude session and ChatGPT, and is concerned that assistants may write in different places, miss one another's instructions, or continue from stale context.

### Binding rule — one room only
1. **Canonical shared room:** `data/AI_COLLABORATION.md` on the repository's `main` branch. This is the only place for cross-assistant communication.
2. Every assistant must read this file first, including the latest dated entries, before starting work. Then inspect the relevant specialized source records and current GitHub state as required by the task.
3. Every assistant must write its CLAIM, IN PROGRESS, DONE, BLOCKED, HANDOFF, review request, owner decision and next action in this file. When replying to another assistant, append the reply here. Do not rely on PR comments, chat history, private notes, another file, or an assumed notification as the handoff.
4. PR comments are reserved for technical review of that specific PR (line-level defects, check evidence and merge discussion). If a PR comment contains a cross-assistant request or durable decision, mirror its substance into this room and link the PR. Do not treat the comment as the shared message.
5. Do not create another file titled or functioning as an operations room, AI handoff, assistant coordination log or parallel task board. Existing specialised records remain in place and are referenced from this room:
   - `data/WORK_LOG.md`: append-only record of completed repository tasks, one line per finished task.
   - `data/PROJECT_STATE.md`: technical/project state and constraints.
   - `data/SEARCH_METRICS.md`: dated measurement evidence and limitations.
   - `docs/ARTICLE-PRODUCTION-QUEUE.md`: qualified article intake and production workflow.
   - Operating Charter, Value Before Publishing, CAO and calculator specifications: governing requirements for their respective subjects.
   These records are not substitutes for the shared room. Do not copy their full contents into this file; summarize only the current decision/status and link to the source.
6. Maintain one latest status per task in the newest dated room entry. Older entries remain historical; if an old instruction conflicts with a newer owner instruction, the newer explicit owner instruction wins. Record supersession clearly instead of silently deleting history.
7. Before editing any shared file, inspect all open PRs and their changed-file overlap. Never overwrite another branch's work. If the room or work log is touched by multiple open PRs, coordinate the merge order, preserve every valid entry and reconcile sequentially. Do not merge a stale/conflicted PR merely to clear the list.
8. No assistant is assumed to run in the background or see a new entry immediately. The next session must read the room at startup. The owner should not have to relay messages manually.

- Repository checked: `home-project-wise/home-project-wise`; canonical room is this file on `main`.
- Latest open PR search returned **#285** (“Record Nov 11 search metrics and cross-cluster research”) and **#283** (“Editorial governance: keep the production queue qualified and balanced”). Both were reported by GitHub as open and `mergeable: false` at the time of inspection. Their PR descriptions indicate documentation/handoff changes; do not merge either stale/conflicted head as-is. Inspect their exact changed files and reconcile against current main before any merge. Preserve all unique research, metrics and owner decisions.

### Required format for all future room updates
- **Date / assistant / status:** CLAIM, IN PROGRESS, DONE, BLOCKED or HANDOFF.
- **Task and owner:** one clearly named task owner.
- **Files / PR / commit:** exact paths and links; say “none” if none.
- **Evidence:** checks actually run and their exact result; distinguish local, PR-head, merged-main and live-site evidence.
- **Overlap / exclusions:** files or articles another assistant must not touch.
- **Next action / recipient:** one concrete next step and who should take it.

The owner supplied a tablet screenshot of a home-renovation calculator. The useful design pattern is not the third-party brand: it is the clear task-first hierarchy — strong calculator heading, selectable project-type cards, grouped options, well-labelled size input/slider, and a prominent primary Calculate action. Do not copy its logo, company name or exact branding.

**Visual brief for Claude — authorized by the owner's request on 2026-11-11:**
- Target: flagship calculator page `website/home-project-calculator.html`; preserve the six existing calculation areas and all current formulas, data contracts and privacy behavior.
- First screen: make the calculator title and concise promise clear; keep the six calculator choices immediately visible and make the selected state unmistakable. Prefer compact selectable cards/chips with a small restrained icon, title and one-line purpose where space allows. Avoid a long decorative hero that pushes the tool below the fold.
- Workspace: present each active calculator as one coherent, high-contrast surface. Group inputs by task/step; align related fields in a responsive grid; make labels, units and helper text readable; expose assumptions before calculation rather than hiding them in tiny text.
- Action/result: one visually dominant Calculate action, full-width on narrow phones; clear validation and loading/disabled states; a distinct result panel with the main estimate first, then useful breakdown/formula/assumption context. Never invent example prices, defaults or outputs.

- Dark mode: every new surface, border, label, selected state, input, button, error and result must have an intentional dark counterpart with readable contrast. Do not rely on browser defaults.
- Accessibility: preserve semantic labels, keyboard operation, visible `:focus-visible`, tab roles/aria state, error association and screen-reader-readable results. Do not use color alone to indicate selection or errors.
- Scope discipline: do not change calculation formulas or add paid data, ZIP pricing, affiliate placements, third-party APIs or server-side collection. Keep user-entered values local as currently documented.

### Mandatory anti-loss protocol — EVERY atomic step
- Before each small edit, test, review or GitHub operation, append **START** with timestamp, task, exact branch, exact file(s)/PR, intended result.
- Immediately after it, append **DONE** with what changed, commit SHA, exact command/test, pass/fail/count, CI run ID/head SHA if relevant, and the next action. If anything blocks progress, append **BLOCKED** with the exact error, what was preserved, and the smallest safe recovery step.
- Keep each step small (one focused edit or test group). Commit and push each coherent milestone before moving to the next. Never leave valuable work only in a local session or unpushed workspace.
- Before any stop — quota, timeout, context limit, interruption, or end of day — append **RESUME HERE** stating current branch + full HEAD SHA, clean/dirty status, changed files, completed items, exact unfinished item, commands/tests last run and results, open PR link/base/head, CI state, and the single next action. This is mandatory even if the session is ending unexpectedly; write the checkpoint before doing any optional cleanup.
- At the start of every fresh session, read the latest RESUME HERE and verify its SHA against GitHub before acting. Never trust stale notes over current repository state.
- Distinguish clearly: locally tested ≠ CI passed ≠ visually inspected ≠ owner-approved/merged ≠ deployed. Never invent evidence or claim success before observing it.

### First message/action for this fresh session
Post a new **RESUME CLAIM + START A** in this room after verifying current PR #293/#298 state. Then execute task A only. Do not ask the owner to repeat context or relay assistant messages. Follow all existing protected-file locks and exclusions; in particular do not edit `scripts/visual_qa.py`, `.github/workflows`, or `website/article-overrides.css` without explicit owner authorization.

## 2026-11-11 — Claude — DONE D1–D3 (read-only findings, no change made) + RESUME HERE

**Status: DONE D1–D3 (~11:44 UTC). Read-only: analysed `website/home-project-calculator.html` at `bd2745a` (= #293 + #298). No code edited, no PR touched, nothing merged.** Evidence level: source reading only; no browser run this step.

- **D1 — Paint/Flooring → Project cost handoff: no handoff exists, and there is no data contract for one.** The Project cost form (`project-form`) takes independent free fields (`project-quantity`, `project-unit-price`, `project-extra`, `project-contingency`, `project-tax`, `project-currency`); Paint and Flooring only print HTML text results and store nothing (page header: "Nothing is stored, logged or sent"). Feeding packs/tins into Project cost would be a new feature (e.g. a button copying the pack count into `project-quantity`), not wiring of an existing contract. Recommendation: do not build under this task; owner/ChatGPT to decide whether the value justifies it. Prices already live inside Paint (`paint-price`) and Flooring (`material-price`), so the cost is already shown per tab.
- **D2 — tab URL hash: read on load only.** The script reads `window.location.hash.slice(1)` once at load (`requestedPanel`) to select a tab (the Node test "calculator supports URL hash tab selection" passes). `selectTab()` and the click/keyboard handlers never write the hash and there is no `hashchange` / `history` call. Effect: deep links like `#panel-material` work, but clicking tabs does not update the address bar, so a copied URL after switching tabs points at the first tab/previous hash. A fix is small (write the hash in `selectTab`) but touches #293-locked code and needs a decision on `replaceState` vs hash, so it waits for #293's outcome.
- **D3 — Paint walls-only scope: stated, but only in the small formula note.** The note under the Paint form says "Ceilings, textured walls, primer and unusual room shapes are not included." The intro line and the result ("Paintable wall area … Buy at least N tin(s)") do not say walls-only, and the note does not mention trim/skirting/doors-frames (only openings are subtracted). Suggested optional change: one line in the Paint intro, "Walls only — ceilings and trim are not included". Not applied (file locked by #293).
- **Next owner:** owner decides D1 scope and whether D2/D3 copy changes are wanted; any change must go on a branch after #293 is merged/closed.

## 2026-11-12 — Claude (design account) — S19: IMAGE BRIEF for the image-prompt agents (ChatGPT and the Claude who write image prompts). ACTION REQUESTED

**Owner decision:** article images stay with the editorial/image workflow and keep improving there. The site front (homepage and shared pages) gets a small set of dedicated images: the image-prompt agents write the prompts here, the owner generates the images, then sends them to the design account, which integrates them. This entry sets the level and the minimum quality bar. Reply in this room with prompt ids.

**Look to match ("quiet luxury", US homeowner audience, readable in Europe too):** editorial interior photography like a print home magazine. Natural window light, soft shadows, 35 mm lens at eye level, light film grain, believable shallow depth of field. Palette: warm linen/cream, deep evergreen #1B2E2A, brass #B98B45, terracotta #A94A1A accents, ink #243238. Real materials (oak, plaster, linen, brushed brass), calm and lived-in, one clear subject, generous negative space.

**American realism:** US wood-frame homes: drywall and wide baseboards, double-hung windows, 36-inch doors, Decora paddle switches, US Type A/B outlets, tape measures in feet and inches, gallon paint cans. Imperial first.

**Never in an image:** visible text, logos or brand marks (a third-party logo on a shirt had to be cropped out of the current planning photo), watermarks, recognisable faces (use hands, backs, silhouettes), copyrighted artworks, cartoon or CGI gloss, HDR, oversaturation, stock clichés (smiling couple with a paint roller), clutter.

**Technical:**
- Hero: 3:4 portrait, at least 1600 x 2133 px. It is cropped into an arch, so keep the subject in the lower 70% with headroom above.
- Planning/feature photo: 7:8 portrait, at least 1400 x 1600 px. Wide: 16:9, at least 1600 x 900. Square tiles: 1:1, at least 1200 px.
- sRGB JPEG or WebP. Hero <= 180 KB (deliver 800/1200/1600 px widths), others <= 120 KB. One consistent colour grade across the set.
- File names lowercase-hyphenated, e.g. `home-hero-arch-living-room.jpg`. Alt text 8-20 words, describes what is visible, no "image of".

**Quality gate (reject and regenerate; max 3 attempts per prompt, then escalate to the owner).** Inspect at 100% zoom and at phone size. FAIL if any of: distorted hands or fingers, melted or merged objects; impossible geometry, bent verticals, wrong-scale doors or windows; duplicated fixtures; plugs, outlets or switches that do not match US shapes; garbled or fake text anywhere; plastic or waxy surfaces; inconsistent shadows or light direction; repeating-pattern artefacts; subject unclear at a glance; visible AI signature or watermark; style drifting from the rest of the set. Each accepted image gets a one-line note here: prompt id, PASS, reviewer.

**Disclosure:** AI-generated images keep the existing caption convention. Design can make the caption quiet and elegant; whether and how to label is an editorial/legal decision for the owner.

**Prompts requested now, in priority order (seed prompts; agents refine and give 3 variations each):**
1. `HOME-HERO` (3:4): "Editorial interior photograph, calm American living room at 8 a.m., low sunlight through a tall double-hung window, a round-arched doorway leading to a hallway, linen sofa in warm oatmeal, one oak side table with a ceramic lamp, plaster walls in soft warm white with a hint of sage, wide oak plank floor, natural wool rug, a single houseplant, no recognisable artwork on the walls, 35 mm lens at eye level, soft film grain, muted palette with deep green and brass accents, realistic materials, uncluttered, portrait 3:4, subject in the lower two thirds with space above." Negative: "text, logo, watermark, people, faces, clutter, cartoon, 3D render, HDR, oversaturated, warped geometry, fake outlets".
2. `HOME-PLANNING` (7:8), replaces the current planning photo: "Editorial close-up of two hands marking a renovation floor plan on large paper on a worn oak table, an architect's scale ruler, a sharpened pencil, a tape measure in feet and inches, a small fan of warm neutral paint swatches, soft window light from the left, shallow depth of field, 50 mm, no faces, no clothing logos, no readable text, portrait 7:8." Same negative prompt.
3. `TASK-FIX`, `TASK-ROOM`, `TASK-PLAN`, `TASK-SMART` (1:1, optional but wanted): hands pressing weatherstripping onto a white interior door frame; a bedroom corner in morning light with linen curtains; a tape measure and a blank sketch pad on oak; a US Decora switch plate and a smart plug in a Type B outlet on a painted wall. Same look and negative prompt.

**Process:** prompt writer posts final prompts with ids here -> owner generates -> an agent runs the quality gate and logs PASS/FAIL here -> owner commits the files to `website/images/` (or sends them to the design account) -> design account integrates, sets `width`/`height`, alt text and responsive sizes, and logs the PR. Existing article images are not renamed or replaced by this work.

The owner says the footer title still did nothing for him: most likely a cached copy on his phone, and he judged it minor now that the header stays in view. His screenshot (6:04) confirms the sticky header works. Evidence: tested = CI; merged = yes; live = yes by content; visually inspected = owner screenshot of the sticky header only.
  **What the research found (sources read in this session; items from roundup articles are secondary and marked):**
  1. *Sticky headers.* Nielsen Norman Group ("Sticky Headers") says to keep a persistent header small (about a tenth of the screen height) and that partially persistent headers (hidden while reading down, shown on scroll up) are most common on mobile. CafeMedia's publisher guidance (citing Google NCI) is at most 50 px tall and hide on scroll down. Smart Interface Design Patterns: reveal when needed, hide when the reader is absorbed. NN/g also reports that hiding navigation behind a menu icon lowers discoverability.
  2. *Our header today (measured from the owner's screenshot):* about 97 CSS px on a phone, roughly 12% of an 800 px screen, because the brand and the Menu button wrap onto two rows. Too tall by these guidelines.
  3. *WCAG 2.2.* SC 2.4.11 Focus Not Obscured (Minimum, AA): a sticky header must not hide the keyboard-focused element; the standard fix is `scroll-padding-top` at least the header height plus a buffer. Ours is 84 px, below the 97 px phone header, so it can fail on phones. SC 2.5.8 Target Size (Minimum, AA): 24 x 24 CSS px; we use 44. (W3C WAI "What's new in WCAG 2.2".)
  4. *Core Web Vitals (web.dev):* LCP <= 2.5 s, INP <= 200 ms, CLS <= 0.1 at the 75th percentile; INP replaced FID in March 2024. We already set image width/height, preload the hero and fonts; no field measurement yet.
  5. *E-E-A-T (Google Search Quality Rater Guidelines, via Search Engine Land and Search Engine Roundtable):* Trust is the centre; Experience, Expertise and Authoritativeness support it. Practical signals named by SEO practitioners: visible author or reviewer credit, About/Contact/Privacy/Terms, regularly updated content, first-hand experience. Our articles show "Published ... Updated ..." and honest AI-image captions, but no author/editorial credit block.
  6. *Competitors (read earlier in S11, plus roundups):* Family Handyman keeps a short top bar and moves categories into a mega menu; This Old House organises navigation by task (plan, estimate cost, find help); calculator-first sites put the form first, then the formula, a worked example, an FAQ, author/reviewer and date, and related calculators. Design publications (Architectural Digest, Remodelista, Houzz, per roundups; secondary): generous white space, large high-quality photography, restrained grids, clear categories, Remodelista positioned as a research tool for people mid-renovation, Houzz with a central search and a simple newsletter sign-up. Limit: I read their text and structure, not their rendered pages.
  **Playbook (principles the design account will apply):** (1) content first, chrome minimal: header at most 56 px on phones, smart sticky; (2) one signature motif (the arch), one accent (brass), one display serif and one sans, nothing else; (3) real tools are the differentiator: every calculator gets formula, worked example, FAQ, related tools and a real "last updated"; (4) trust is design: author or editorial credit, dates, sources, honest AI labels, policy links; (5) performance budget: LCP <= 2.5 s (target 2.0 s on 4G), CLS <= 0.1, INP <= 200 ms, measured not assumed; (6) accessibility budget: WCAG 2.2 AA including Focus Not Obscured; (7) photography standard S19; (8) every entry states tested / merged / live / visually inspected.
  **Backlog from the study (nothing started; the owner paused building):** P0 small and in the design scope, doable on initiative: compact phone header (brand and Menu on one row, at most 56 px), `scroll-padding-top` = header height + 16 px, hide-on-scroll-down and show-on-scroll-up for the sticky header, keep "Calculator" visible next to Menu on the homepage phone header. P1 needs editorial coordination: author or editorial credit block; real "Last updated" on tools; calculator pages with worked example and related calculators. P2 structural: one shared navigation across generated pages via the build script; central search entry. P3 measurement: get a Lighthouse/PageSpeed baseline for home, an article, the library and the calculator before and after each change.
- **S38 done — owner request: the header must not stay fixed all the time; it should disappear while reading and come back when the page is moved (Stage 6, the P0 batch of the S37 playbook).** Behaviour chosen: hide after about 24 px of downward scroll, show after about 12 px of upward scroll, always shown near the top of the page, never hidden while the homepage menu is open or while a link inside the header has focus; it slides with a short transition (none with reduced motion). The owner's words ("returns when I move the page up or down") could also mean it should reappear on a downward scroll; the standard pattern (research S37: NN/g partially persistent header, CafeMedia 50 px / hide on scroll down) was implemented and the owner will say if he wants it to reappear after a pause as well. **How:** new `website/header.js` (idempotent, silent on failure); `ensure_head_tags()` in `scripts/rebuild_site_indexes.py` adds `<script src="[prefix]header.js" defer>` to the head of every page at build (it already runs over all pages and all articles), so no page was edited by hand; CSS in `article-overrides.css` (`.nav-hidden`, transition, `scroll-padding-top: calc(var(--hdr-h,84px) + 16px)` where the script sets `--hdr-h` to the real header height, fixing the 84 px vs 97 px mismatch found in S37); `home.css`: on phones the homepage header is one row of about 56 px (logo 28 px high, Menu beside it) instead of two rows of about 97 px. **Status:** PR for branch `claude-design/2026-11-13-smart-header` opened in this commit; CI, merge and live result are recorded in S39 in the next batch. Next step for the owner or any agent with an API key: run https://pagespeed.web.dev on the homepage, one article, the guide library and the calculator (mobile) and paste the scores here; or add a PageSpeed API key as a repository secret if the team wants this automated. **Substitute audit (HTTP, all 51 sitemap pages):** every page has `lang`, viewport, `main`, canonical and exactly one `h1`, with no heading-level skips; 0 pages are free of every flagged item. Findings: (a) 51 pages load every image eagerly (no `loading="lazy"`); (b) 50 pages have an image without `width`/`height` (mainly the header/footer wordmark), a layout-shift risk; (c) 18 pages (hubs, about, policies, tools) have no `og:image`; (d) 17 articles have a `<title>` over 65 characters and 4 pages a description over 170 (editorial, not touched); (e) 5 pages (library, editorial policy, privacy, terms, contact) have no JSON-LD (not touched, proposal); (f) 4 tool pages have inputs with no `id` hook for a label (needs a read of each page, not touched); (g) 147 unique images weigh 8.5 MB in total, 11 article hero images are 210-235 KB (editorial assets, not touched; WebP would save roughly a third, proposal). **Done in this batch (all in the build script, applied at build to every page and article; no page edited by hand):** `enhance_images()` reserves the wordmark's space through `aspect-ratio:642/96` (not width/height attributes, which could stretch the logo where CSS does not set `width:auto`) and adds `loading="lazy" decoding="async"` to every image after the first two (the header logo and the main image stay eager); pages without `og:image` get the new share image and `twitter:card=summary_large_image`. **Tested before committing:** the function was run over the live HTML of all 51 pages: image count unchanged, no duplicated attributes, idempotent on a second pass, first two images untouched; {sum(r[2] for r in res)} of {sum(r[3] for r in res)} images become lazy. **Status:** PR opened in this commit; CI, merge and live result to be recorded in S41. ## 2026-11-12 — ChatGPT A — Owner-approved hub-and-spoke editorial strategy

**Decision:** The owner approved adopting a pillar / hub-and-spoke model across HomeProjectWise. The operating charter now contains Section 15, defining hub roles, specialist spokes, contextual bidirectional links, cluster mapping and rules for deciding when a question deserves its own URL.

**Competitive patterns reviewed:**
- The Spruce's home-improvement navigation separates broad areas (e.g. Kitchen, Bathroom, Painting, Exteriors) and surfaces specialist topic links inside articles; the inspected apartment-gardening article links directly to container gardening, sunlight, potting mix, grow lights and related specialist answers in context. This is a useful pattern: answer the immediate question, then offer the next relevant depth link.
- This Old House's Home Improvement A–Z organizes many home areas and breaks each into task families such as budget, how-to/DIY, installation, maintenance, planning, renovation, safety and tools/materials. This suggests a reusable taxonomy for our hubs rather than a flat pile of articles.
- Search Engine Journal's hub-and-spoke guidance supports contextual links between a broad hub and focused pages, without forcing a page to link to dozens of unrelated pages.

**Implementation rules:**
1. Do not turn every subsection into a URL. A spoke must solve a distinct homeowner task / search intent with enough depth to stand alone.
2. A hub must give useful orientation and classify solution paths, not just list links.
3. Link hub → spokes at the relevant decision point; spoke → hub for context; spoke ↔ spoke only when the next task is genuinely related.
4. A cluster can contain many spokes, including dozens when justified, but group them by subtopic or decision stage and avoid an overwhelming link dump.
5. Existing useful URLs should be mapped and linked in place; do not mass-rewrite, duplicate or migrate URLs to impose the new model.
6. Add cluster ID, hub/spoke role, parent ID/URL, intent, overlap check and proposed internal links to new briefs / queue records. Audit existing inventory for clusters and missing links.
7. This architecture must accelerate useful coverage, not become a publication gate. Continue the daily production target while building clusters in parallel.

**Competitor references checked 2026-11-12:**
- The Spruce article structure: https://www.thespruce.com/the-ultimate-guide-to-urban-gardening-7555044
- This Old House topic taxonomy: https://www.thisoldhouse.com/home-improvement
- Search Engine Journal hub/spoke linking: https://www.searchenginejournal.com/hub-spoke-internal-links/442005/

**Status:** Strategy recorded in the operating charter and this operations room. No existing articles, URLs, queue assignments or open PRs were changed by this strategy entry. Next ChatGPT A action: audit the existing portfolio and current queue for the first 2–3 practical clusters, map current pages to hubs/spokes, identify missing links and specialist gaps, then add prioritized cluster briefs without disturbing reserved Claude assignments or design ownership.


## 2026-11-12 — ChatGPT A — Owner-approved continuous initiative principle

**Owner decision:** Adopted the principle of continuous initiative, creativity and evidence-led improvement as a mandatory operating standard for ChatGPT and Claude. This is not a motivational slogan: it defines expected behaviour, a repeatable initiative loop, autonomy boundaries and proactive reporting.

**Required behaviour:** Collaborators must actively inspect the project and market, identify opportunities and risks, challenge assumptions, rank recommendations by long-term value, explain evidence and uncertainty, and take safe in-scope action instead of waiting for the owner to provide every task. Bring forward practical, evidence-backed improvements across content, SEO/CAO, UX, calculators, technology, distribution, trust and monetization. Do not flood the owner with unranked ideas.

**Autonomy:** Research, analysis, briefs, prototypes, non-destructive checks and in-scope reversible documentation work can proceed proactively. Owner approval remains required for merge/deploy, live publishing or material live changes, URL/architecture/navigation/brand changes, commercial spend or affiliate changes, sensitive/security/privacy matters, irreversible actions, strategic priority changes and work in another collaborator's protected ownership. Initiative never overrides safety, quality, evidence integrity or owner authority.

**Operating loop:** Observe → Diagnose → Propose → Act (within authority) → Verify → Learn and record. Distinguish proposed / implemented / tested / merged / deployed / live-verified. At meaningful checkpoints report the strongest opportunity, key risk, evidence, action taken, approval needed and next highest-value step.

**Long-term objective:** Build HomeProjectWise into a useful, trusted, distinctive and commercially sustainable home-improvement resource with strong homeowner outcomes, tools, UX, organic and AI-search visibility, engagement and conversion—not simply a larger site.

**Status:** The principle is now Section 16 of the operating charter and recorded here in the central operations room. ChatGPT A and Claude collaborators are expected to follow it; future handoffs should explicitly require the incoming account to read and apply Section 16. No production code, live content, merge or deployment was changed by this policy update.

## 2026-11-12 — Owner decision: article-first image creation (replaces prompt handoff to owner)

The owner approved a better workflow: the image collaborator must read the actual article and independently create professional images that accurately express its subject and add visual value. The owner does not want to receive prompts to paste into a separate ChatGPT image conversation.

**New binding standard:** see Section 17 of `docs/HOMEPROJECTWISE-OPERATING-CHARTER-2026-11-10.md` (commit `1088c749c65154e72edc14af2b306f302bf36655`).

**Operating change:**
- Read the complete article first; understand the reader task, examples, limitations and safety constraints.
- Plan internally the hero plus only the supporting visuals that genuinely clarify distinct parts of the article.
- Choose photography, diagram or illustration based on what best explains the subject.
- Generate the actual image assets directly. Prompts remain internal production notes; do not ask the owner to copy/paste prompts or act as the image-production operator.
- Reject generic attractive room scenes that do not communicate the article's specific topic. The image must make the subject clear at a glance and provide explanatory value as well as editorial beauty.
- Ground every visible detail in the article or reliable references. No invented dimensions, labels, equipment details, unsafe demonstrations or unsupported outcomes.
- Inspect full size and phone size; iterate to fix diagnosed defects, not merely rephrase prompts. Maximum three meaningful attempts before escalation.
- Record asset ID, article/section, dimensions, format, alt text, reviewer, PASS/FAIL and limitations. Confirm the actual asset is present in the PR/repository before claiming it is committed.
- Keep S19 quiet-luxury quality and US-home realism. Design account retains site-front/brand image ownership; article images stay with the editorial/image workflow.
- Owner receives reviewed finished assets or a specific exception requiring a decision—not routine image prompts.

**Immediate consequence for EN-001 and HVAC-001:** their current prompt-only hero files are not finished image deliverables. The assigned image collaborator must read PR #318 and #319 article drafts, generate article-specific hero candidates (and assess whether each diagram genuinely helps), inspect them at full and phone size, then hand off actual assets and QA evidence. This does not authorize merge or deployment. Also retain the outstanding HVAC unrelated-link correction and the EN mobile log check.

**Verification status:** policy added to the operating charter; operations-room entry added here. No article images were generated or committed by this documentation update.
